A Regime-Switching Approach to the Unbalanced Schrödinger Bridge Problem
// Restore the saved color scheme preference, or
  // enact the browser preference if "automatic", 
  // without expecting DOM load to have completed.
  //
  // Also restore any saved readingmode and ToC display preferences.
  function initializeReadingPreferences() {
    let saved_theme = localStorage.getItem("ar5iv_theme") || "automatic";
    if (saved_theme === "automatic") {
      if (window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches) {
        saved_theme = "dark";
      }
    }
    if (saved_theme == "dark") {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-theme", "dark");
    } else {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-theme", "light");
    }

    const tocDisplay = localStorage.getItem('arxiv_html_paper_toc_display');
    if (tocDisplay) {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-toc-display", tocDisplay);
    }
    const readingMode = localStorage.getItem('arxiv_html_paper_reading_mode');
    if (readingMode) {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-reading-mode", readingMode);
    }
    // Pre-apply spinout-banner dismissal here, before the banner paints, so it
    // never flashes in only to be hidden later by the deferred arxiv-header.js.
    // Key matches arxiv-header.js: "arxiv-banner-dismissed:" + data-banner-name.
    if (localStorage.getItem('arxiv-banner-dismissed:spinout-nonprofit')) {
      document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-banner-dismissed", "");
    }
  }
  // Run as soon as JS starts, to minimize repainting
  initializeReadingPreferences();
/* Banner pre-dismissal (set above before paint -> no flash-then-hide) and
     reading-mode chrome hiding. */
  html[data-banner-dismissed] .ds-announcement { display: none; }
  html[data-reading-mode="enabled"] .ds-announcement,
  html[data-reading-mode="enabled"] .ds-site-footer { display: none; }
  /* Print keeps the reader header's logo, not its controls: the theme's own print rule
     (header, nav { display: none }) sits in a layer that its header layer outranks. */
  @media print { .arxiv-html-header .html-header-nav { display: none; } }
  /* Keep the announcement text dark on the Open-Blue band in both colour themes
     (otherwise it inherits the paper's warm-wash text in dark mode and washes out). */
  .ds-announcement-text { color: var(--arxiv-ink, #1c1a17); }

arXiv:2512.12971v1 [math.PR] 15 Dec 2025
A Regime-Switching Approach to the Unbalanced Schrödinger Bridge Problem
Thanks:

Andrei Zlotchevski
1,†
and
Linan Chen
1,†,∗

Abstract.
The unbalanced Schrödinger bridge problem (uSBP) seeks to interpolate between a probability measure
ρ
0
\rho_{0}
and a sub-probability measure
ρ
T
\rho_{T}
while minimizing KL divergence to a reference measure
𝐑
\mathbf{R}
on a path space. In this work, we investigate the case where
𝐑
\mathbf{R}
is the path measure of a diffusion process with killing, which we interpret as a regime-switching diffusion. In addition to matching the initial and terminal distributions of trajectories that survive up to time
T
T
, we consider a general constraint
ψ
⁡
(
t
,
x
)
\psi(t,x)
on the distribution of killing times and/or killing locations.
We investigate the uSBPs corresponding to four choices of
ψ
\psi
in detail which reflect different levels of information available to an observer. We also provide a rigorous analysis of the connections and the comparisons among the outcomes of these four cases. Our results are novel in the field of uSBP. The regime-switching approach proposed in this work provides a unified framework for tackling different uSBP scenarios, which not only reconciles but also extends the existing literature on uSBP.
Key words and phrases:
unbalanced Schrödinger bridge, regime-switching diffusion, diffusion process with killing, stochastic control, KL divergence
1991 Mathematics Subject Classification
35Q93, 60H10, 60H30, 60J60, 93E20, 94A17
1.
Introduction
This paper is dedicated to the unbalanced Schrödinger bridge problem (uSBP) for diffusion processes. Roughly speaking, given probability distributions
ρ
0
\rho_{0}
and
ρ
T
\rho_{T}
the classic Schrödinger bridge problem (SBP) aims to “bridge” the two distributions in a way that minimizes KL divergence (relative entropy) to a reference path measure
𝐑
\mathbf{R}
. The unbalanced Schrödinger bridge problem, on the other hand, considers the case where
ρ
T
\rho_{T}
is a sub-probability measure (i.e. with total mass less than 1). In this Introduction, we shall describe the type of reference measure under consideration and then rigorously formulate the uSBP as a classic SBP with regime switching.
There are two main objectives to this work. First, we seek to establish a fundamental theory of the diffusion uSBP that can model a broad range of constraints imposed on the distribution of killed paths. By applying the theory of the SBP for (jump) diffusions with regime switching
[
17
]
, we will thoroughly investigate the uSBP for diffusions and derive many of its properties in full generality. Then, we aim to connect the existing uSBP literature
[
3
,
6
,
5
]
under the regime-switching framework adopted in this work and demonstrate how these different problems are directly related. In fact, our approach will allow us to recover the existing results from other uSBP research and to offer new insight on some aspects of the uSBP.

1.2.
Background and Motivation
In the classic SBP, a large number of particles is observed at times
t
=
0
t=0
and
t
=
T
t=T
to have spatial distributions
ρ
0
\rho_{0}
and
ρ
T
\rho_{T}
respectively. One must then find the “most probable way” that the particles have evolved between the two observations, which is understood as finding the path measure
𝐏
^
\widehat{\mathbf{P}}
that minimizes the KL divergence to the reference measure (prior belief)
𝐑
\mathbf{R}
. In particular, the SBP for diffusion processes is a long-celebrated problem dating back to E. Schrödinger’s papers
[
15
,
16
]
in the 1930s and it has generated a rich literature; a selection of works include
[
1
,
11
,
12
,
8
,
7
,
4
,
14
,
2
]
.
In the unbalanced SBP, the number of particles observed at time
T
T
is expected to be less than that at time
0
0
. Therefore, one must reconstruct the “most likely paths” while accounting for the disappearance, or the killing, of the missing particles. The original work
[
3
]
on uSBP treated this problem by considering an augmentation of the state space to include an absorbing “cemetery state”
𝔠
\mathfrak{c}
. The killing of a particle was interpreted as a jump to
𝔠
\mathfrak{c}
, and the problem was recast as a classic SBP over càdlàg paths in
ℝ
d
∪
{
𝔠
}
\mathbb{R}^{d}\cup\left\{\mathfrak{c}\right\}
. In this paper, we instead adopt a
regime-switching
approach to this problem, following the example in
[
17
]
.
The advantage of our approach is two-fold: first, we can directly apply the general theory of the SBP for regime-switching (jump) diffusions developed in
[
17
]
to study the uSBP, and second, we have additional flexibility in the use of the killing time
𝔎
\mathfrak{K}
and the spatial coordinate
X
𝔎
−
X_{\mathfrak{K}-}
for a killed particle, allowing us to model a variety of real-life situations with different types of observational data. We elaborate more on the latter point below.
Although, as in the classic SBP framework, we are only allowed to make two observations on the path: first at time
0
0
, and then at time
T
T
, in our regime-switching setup, we are able to incorporate more information about the path than just what the endpoints can tell seemingly. This is due to the fact that any killed particle is frozen in “time and space”. For example, if
ψ
⁡
(
t
,
x
)
=
x
\psi(t,x)=x
, then the spatial component
X
T
X_{T}
not only gives the position at time
T
T
of a particle that
survived
, but also records the killing location of a dead particle. As a result, the terminal constraint
ρ
T
​
(
⋅
,
⊗
)
\rho_{T}(\cdot,\otimes)
exactly prescribes the distribution of killing locations.
Let us also highlight the pair of papers by Eldesoukey
et al.
[
6
,
5
]
that treat the cases when certain target distributions are imposed on the killed particles. In contrast, the uSBPs we treat in this paper also include target distributions on the particles that survived. We will draw connections to the above mentioned uSBP works in
Section
5
.
1.3.
The Four Scenarios
In this work, we focus on four uSBP models that are highly relevant in applications, and we will examine various aspects of the Schrödinger bridge obtained for these models on a case-by-case basis.
To begin, in all of our uSBP models, we assume that the observer knows the initial distribution
ρ
0
​
(
⋅
,
⊚
∙
)
\rho_{0}(\cdot,\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\circledcirc$\cr$\displaystyle\bullet$\crcr}}{}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\circledcirc$\cr$\textstyle\bullet$\crcr}}{}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\circledcirc$\cr$\scriptstyle\bullet$\crcr}}{}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\circledcirc$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\crcr}}{}}})
, as well as the distribution of particles that survived up to time
T
T
, which is given by
ρ
T
​
(
⋅
,
⊚
∙
)
\rho_{T}(\cdot,\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\circledcirc$\cr$\displaystyle\bullet$\crcr}}{}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\circledcirc$\cr$\textstyle\bullet$\crcr}}{}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\circledcirc$\cr$\scriptstyle\bullet$\crcr}}{}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\circledcirc$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\crcr}}{}}})
.
As for the killed particles, we investigate four different choices of
ψ
\psi
, to which we refer as
Scenarios
of the uSBP. These choices reflect the information available to the observer regarding the killed particles:
(1)
The observer knows the joint distribution of killing locations and killing times.
(2)
The observer knows the distribution of killing times only.
(3)
The observer knows the distribution of killing locations only.
(4)
The observer knows the total mass of killed particles, with no knowledge of its distribution.
As alluded to above, the types of constraints on the distribution of killed particles concerned in Scenarios (1), (2) and (3) have appeared in the uSBP literature
[
6
,
5
]
, but these works did not impose a target distribution

​
(
⋅

⊚
∙

for the surviving particles. The regime-switching approach will allow us to solve these uSBP scenarios in full generality. Scenario (4) is the original uSBP
[
3
]

Main Results
Our work is composed of two main parts. In the first part, we derive various fundamental properties of the unbalanced Schrödinger bridge
𝐏
^
\widehat{\mathbf{P}}
for diffusion with killing by adapting the general theory from
[

𝐏

\widehat{\mathbf{P}}

φ

\widehat{\varphi}

\widehat{\mathbf{P}}

3.
The Four Scenarios: Case-by-Case Results

5.2.

Instructions for reporting errors
We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
      support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
      methods listed below:
Click the "Report Issue"
(
)
button, located in the page header.
Tip:
You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.
Our team has already identified
the following issues
. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
      may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
      should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
      all.
Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a
list of packages that need conversion
, and welcome
developer contributions
.
